# Solace-AI MVP Bug Review (2026-10-17)

This review replaces `docs/READINESS-REVIEW-FABLE.md` for MVP triage. It covers **identification only**; fix planning comes later.

**How it was produced**

- Models: Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5.
- **Fable re-verification:** each of Fable's 128 findings was checked against the code on disk by one Sonnet and one Opus verifier working independently. When they disagreed (on whether it is real, or on severity by 2+ levels), an Opus tie-breaker decided. That happened 10 times.
- **Bug hunt:** 17 area and cross-cutting hunters ran a first round, and 10 of them a second round with the other model. Seven scopes got one round only: safety, inter-service contracts, clinical/crisis logic, schema vs SQL, build/deploy, async/concurrency, and error handling. The original run was interrupted and the rerun trimmed. A coverage critic then proposed 3 gap hunt(s): crisis-event-plane-delivery-semantics, conversation-storage-durability-and-at-rest-protection, single-host-ops-alerting-and-caddy-fronted-behavior. Together they produced 438 candidates.
- **Deduplication:** an Opus pass merged the candidates into 277 groups. 30 of them matched a real Fable finding and were folded in as corroboration.
- **New-finding panel:** each of the remaining 247 new findings went to a refute panel. A Sonnet voter checked reachability and mechanism; an Opus voter checked overall correctness and severity. When they split, an Opus tie-breaker cast a third vote. A majority refute removed a finding: 243 were upheld and 4 removed.
- **Evidence:** file:line citations refer to the working tree, which is HEAD 257aa9d plus uncommitted changes. It has not changed since 2026-10-12.

**MVP scope used for severity (assumed; please correct if wrong)**

Assumed MVP = first limited pilot release. Real end users sign up and log in (email+password and Google OAuth), then chat with the companion through the orchestrator REST/WebSocket API. Every message is safety-screened; a detected crisis shows crisis resources AND alerts an on-call human clinician. Therapy, diagnosis, personality and memory services contribute to replies. User data is persisted durably and PHI is protected (encryption at rest, per-user isolation, working account deletion). Deployed single-region on one host via docker compose (infrastructure/aws-staging) with health checks and basic monitoring/alerting. OUT of MVP scope: multi-AZ HA/DR, horizontal scaling, load/chaos testing, SOC2 evidence, MFA, the Kong api_gateway package, ClickHouse analytics, config_service as a product feature, enterprise RBAC/ABAC. Clinical validation sign-off and BAAs are owner decisions, but defects that create direct user-safety or privacy risk ARE in scope.

**Severity levels**

- **blocker:** cannot ship.
- **high:** fix before MVP unless the owner explicitly accepts it.
- **medium:** should fix for MVP or shortly after.
- **low:** minor.
- **post-mvp:** real, but only matters beyond MVP scope.
- **none:** not a defect.

## 1. Executive summary

- **Verdict: not ready for the MVP pilot.** The stack cannot be built, booted or migrated from the repository as it stands. If those faults were fixed, the crisis path would still fail at several independent points, so a user in crisis may see no resources, and no clinician is alerted.
- **Register: 344 verified entries.** 35 blocker, 72 high, 123 medium, 102 low and 12 post-MVP. Entries from Fable's review hold 15 of the blockers and 21 of the highs. Entries from the new hunt hold 20 blockers and 51 highs.
- **Build and boot.** Every image build fails on a missing requirements-runtime.txt (MVP-032), and the pins cannot resolve once it is recreated (MVP-005). Notification-service cannot import (MVP-006, MVP-012), and HEAD is internally inconsistent (MVP-008). Under ENVIRONMENT=staging or production, compose and runbook values crash user-service and all 10 services (MVP-004, MVP-031, MVP-010). The migration chain rolls back to zero tables (MVP-001, MVP-009).
- **Crisis detection.** Chat ingress screening is a 14-phrase keyword list that misses common phrasings, curly apostrophes and homicidal or abuse disclosures (MVP-017, MVP-025, MVP-026). LLM crisis classification is skipped or not routed (MVP-023, MVP-024). The safety service's LLM layer is unkeyed, and its fallback demotes HIGH crises to LOW or ELEVATED (MVP-030).
- **Clinician alerting.** The orchestrator cannot reach Kafka and its outbox is never drained (MVP-003, MVP-014, MVP-015). The event plane is off in shipped config (MVP-028). A failed alert is never redelivered (MVP-007). Alerts go to a placeholder address because the roster lookup always fails and no supported path creates a clinician account (MVP-011, MVP-105).
- **Service wiring was never exercised end to end.** Orchestrator agent clients call localhost, send no credentials, and disagree with the target schemas and enum case (MVP-020, MVP-021, MVP-022). Graph reducers replay stale replies and lock a thread into crisis mode after one flag (MVP-018, MVP-019). Service SQL does not match the migrated schema in user, memory, personality and therapy (MVP-034, MVP-060, MVP-061, MVP-080, MVP-096).
- **PHI controls exist in code but do not run.** Field encryption is a no-op for raw SQL writes (MVP-055). The audit logger is never called (MVP-091). All services connect as a superuser that bypasses RLS (MVP-041). The log sanitizer passes free text (MVP-093). Account deletion never leaves user-service (MVP-029).
- **Failures are silent.** Errors become templates or in-memory fallbacks while /health stays green (MVP-076, MVP-069, MVP-107). No service serves metrics and no alerting exists (MVP-036, MVP-044). CI has never run a test (MVP-038), and no test runs against real infrastructure (MVP-039).
- **Fable was accurate on facts.** 127 of its 128 findings are real: 82 confirmed, 45 partial (claim corrected) and 1 refuted (F-044). Its reviewer note warned of a 1-in-3 false-positive rate, and that warning was wrong. The redis_url finding it rejected was upheld by both verifiers as a blocker (MVP-016).
- **Fable's severities were poorly calibrated, and its list repeats itself.** 7 of its P2/P3 findings are MVP blockers (MVP-002, MVP-006, MVP-007, MVP-011, MVP-030). 4 of its 9 P0 findings are not blockers (MVP-055, MVP-065, MVP-091, MVP-112). 26 of its 128 findings duplicate others, so they collapse to 101 register entries.
- **The new hunt found most of the remaining risk.** 438 candidates were grouped into 277. 30 groups folded into Fable entries, 247 were verified as new, 243 were upheld and 4 were killed. The upheld entries include defect classes Fable did not cover at all: inter-service contract drift (MVP-021, MVP-022), graph-state reducers (MVP-018, MVP-019) and code-versus-schema mismatches (MVP-034, MVP-060, MVP-096). They also add many crisis-detection misses that Fable did not report (MVP-023 to MVP-026).


### Register totals

| blocker | high | medium | low | post-mvp | none | total |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 35 | 72 | 123 | 102 | 12 | 0 | 344 |

## 2. MVP readiness by journey

### Signup and login: broken
Under ENVIRONMENT=staging or production, user-service crash-loops on the hardcoded USER_SERVICE_ENV (MVP-004) and on the missing SECRET_KEY and ENCRYPTION_KEY (MVP-031). Its database connections fail on the SSL default (MVP-010) and on repository pools that point at localhost (MVP-033). The schema is never created (MVP-001, MVP-009). Once it exists, the users INSERT omits NOT NULL columns, so no account can be created by password or Google (MVP-034). Verification email can never be delivered (MVP-035). Google sign-in cannot hand tokens to a web or mobile client (MVP-104), and its email linking allows pre-account takeover (MVP-102). There is no password reset (MVP-099). Once the rate limiter loads, all clients share Caddy's IP bucket, so about 21 bad logins a minute lock everyone out (MVP-101).

### Chat turn: broken
The orchestrator crashes at import under ENVIRONMENT=production (MVP-013, MVP-016). In any environment it does not start while Weaviate's healthcheck fails (MVP-002). Once running, every agent call goes to localhost with no credentials and with payloads the services reject (MVP-020, MVP-021, MVP-022). Therapy, diagnosis and personality then return canned templates that are reported as success (MVP-076, MVP-078). LLM provider keys are read under names the docs do not give (MVP-057). From the second turn on, replies repeat earlier replies (MVP-018). The chat LLM sees no session history or stored memory (MVP-065, MVP-073). WS clients built from the API handoff doc have their frames dropped with no error (MVP-072).

### Crisis detection to clinician alert: broken
Detection misses many real disclosures. The default screen is a 14-phrase keyword list (MVP-017) that curly apostrophes defeat (MVP-025) and that has no homicidal or abuse coverage (MVP-026). LLM crisis classification is skipped or not routed (MVP-023, MVP-024). The safety LLM layer is unkeyed and demotes HIGH crises (MVP-030). When the keyword precheck matches, the orchestrator shows crisis resources. The alert then stops. The orchestrator is not on the data network, and its outbox is never drained (MVP-003, MVP-014, MVP-015). The event plane is disabled in shipped config (MVP-028). Notification-service cannot boot (MVP-006, MVP-012). A failed alert is never redelivered (MVP-007). Recipient lookup always falls back to a placeholder address (MVP-011), and no supported path creates a clinician account (MVP-105). One crisis flag, including a keyword false positive, also locks the thread into the crisis reply and re-emits a crisis event every turn (MVP-019).

### Data persistence: broken
Migrations are never applied and cannot apply (MVP-001, MVP-009). Services that should use Postgres point at localhost or fail the TLS handshake (MVP-033, MVP-010). Some then fail outright, and others drop to in-memory stores (MVP-088, MVP-052). Against a migrated database, writes and reads do not match the schema in user, memory, personality and therapy (MVP-034, MVP-060, MVP-061, MVP-080, MVP-096), and memory still answers stored=true. Conversation checkpoints live only in process memory (MVP-066). Therapy and diagnosis Redis sessions never initialise (MVP-095). No backup exists for any PHI store (MVP-037).

### PHI protection and per-user isolation: broken
PHI is written in plaintext because field encryption only covers ORM writes (MVP-055). The documented key recipe also leaves encryption off in staging (MVP-092). All services connect as the Postgres superuser, so RLS is bypassed (MVP-041). Any authenticated user can resume another user's thread and read its transcript (MVP-070). The Portkey semantic cache can serve one user's personalised reply to another (MVP-056). Any user can list clinician contact details (MVP-103). No audit trail is written (MVP-091). The log sanitizer passes free-text disclosures into host logs (MVP-093). Consent is recorded but never enforced (MVP-100). Redis and Grafana stay published on the host in the prod overlay (MVP-040).

### Account deletion: broken
DELETE /users/me returns 204 and revokes sessions in user-service. user.deleted is never published or consumed, so PHI survives in the other PHI-holding services (MVP-029). The per-service erasure paths would fail if called. Memory and therapy hit tables that do not exist (MVP-062, MVP-097), the safety module targets missing tables (MVP-089), and the orchestrator has no erase path for checkpoints (MVP-071). The event outbox keeps plaintext PHI forever (MVP-054). A partial failure cannot be retried (MVP-216).

### Build, deploy and boot: broken
No image builds (MVP-032), and the dependency set does not resolve once the missing file is recreated (MVP-005). A git clone of HEAD is internally inconsistent (MVP-008). Notification and personality cannot boot (MVP-006, MVP-012, MVP-027). Weaviate never becomes healthy (MVP-002). All 10 services crash under staging or production because two required secrets are set and documented nowhere (MVP-031). The AWS staging template admits no inbound traffic, so Caddy is unreachable and cannot obtain certificates (MVP-043). The CI image build uses the wrong context and has never run (MVP-112).

### Operability (health, monitoring, alerting): broken
None of the 10 services serves /metrics by default, Prometheus scrapes wrong targets, and no alert rules or Alertmanager exist (MVP-036). The host has no alarms or auto-recovery (MVP-044), and unhealthy containers are never restarted (MVP-119). /health stays green while the event bridge, LLM, agents, consumers or datastores are down (MVP-160, MVP-110, MVP-153, MVP-107). The orchestrator logs crisis events as emitted when nothing received them (MVP-069). Logs exist only as rotating per-container json-file output (MVP-114) and carry no tracebacks (MVP-180). The single Kafka broker is likely to be OOM-killed under the prod memory cap (MVP-045).


## 3. How Fable's review held up

| Verdict | Count |

| partial | 45 |

**Fable P-level vs. verified MVP severity**

| Fable | blocker | high | medium | low | post-mvp | none | refuted | fixed | unverified |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P0 | 5 | 3 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| P1 | 9 | 16 | 11 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| P2 | 5 | 7 | 14 | 18 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| P3 | 2 | 2 | 4 | 20 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

### Accuracy
Fable reported 128 findings. Verification confirmed 82, marked 45 partial (real defect, claim corrected) and refuted 1 (F-044). So 127 of 128 describe a real defect. Sonnet and Opus agreed on 118 verdicts, and 10 needed an Opus tie-break. Fable's 26 internal duplicates reduce the 127 real findings to 101 register entries.

### What it got right
Fable found many of the faults that stop the pilot outright. These include the missing requirements-runtime.txt it ranked first (MVP-032), migrations that are never applied (MVP-001), the Weaviate healthcheck (MVP-002), images missing infrastructure/ (MVP-013), the disabled crisis event plane (MVP-028), bridges with no poller (MVP-014), swallowed fail-loud guards (MVP-015), deletion that never fans out (MVP-029), no-op PHI encryption (MVP-055), the unused audit logger (MVP-091), no backups (MVP-037), no alerting (MVP-036) and gaps in CI test coverage (MVP-038). Its entries hold 15 of the 35 blockers and 21 of the 72 highs. The reviewer note grouped several items under "Exists in code but not wired for deployment". Verification found that pattern in many areas.

### The single false positive: F-044
F-044 said transformer sentiment inference, at 50-300 ms of CPU per message, blocks the safety-service event loop on the crisis path. That cannot happen. sentiment_analyzer.py imports transformers only when torch.cuda.is_available() is true, so the CPU transformer branch is dead code. No image installs torch, transformers or vader. The analyzer runs in lexicon-only mode, a dict-lookup scan bounded by the 10,000-character request limit. Fable's finding cites the synchronous call inside async code. It does not account for the import guard or for the dependency set the images ship. The verifiers split, and the Opus tie-break refuted it.

### How the reviewer note misjudged redis_url, and what "partial" meant
The reviewer note spot-checked three items and rejected one. It treated that result as a 1-in-3 false-positive rate. The rejected item was the orchestrator `settings.redis_url` finding (F-019/F-020). The note said the field is defined at config.py:80. That field belongs to PersistenceSettings, which is reached through get_config().persistence(). main.py reads OrchestratorAppSettings, which has no redis_url. The AttributeError is swallowed and the Redis client is None. Under ENVIRONMENT=production the rate limiter then raises at import, and the orchestrator cannot boot. Both verifiers upheld it as a blocker, with a repro (MVP-016). All three spot-checked items turned out real: F-001 (blocker), F-019/F-020 (blocker) and F-017 (medium, MVP-108). The verified false-positive rate is 1 in 128. The note also called the spot-check a P0 check, but F-019/F-020 were P1. It also dismissed F-029, the large uncommitted tree, as "by design". Verification kept that finding as a blocker (MVP-008), because HEAD 257aa9d is internally inconsistent and a git-clone deploy crash-loops analytics and config.

"Partial" (45 findings) mostly meant that the defect was real but part of Fable's explanation was wrong:
- Wrong mechanism. F-090 blamed the idempotency claim, but the live cause is FAILED offsets committed with no RETRY (MVP-007). F-057 blamed dead code for the missing 0.85 floor, but main.py passes a stale 0.9 (MVP-083). F-077 blamed a missing key for keyword-only retrieval, but no read path runs vector search.
- Wrong location. F-089 cited the dead websocket.py ConnectionManager instead of the live api.py handler. F-079 cited the repository factory instead of the live MetricsStore. F-072 named the wrong client class.
- Stale details. F-029 gave 111/88 files where the tree now has 85/77.
- Overstated reach or impact. Examples are F-050, F-083, F-098, F-106, F-118, F-120 and F-127.
- Understated impact. F-032 said 9 of 10 services lack /metrics, and the true figure is 10 of 10. F-045 said some suites are missing from CI, and in fact CI has never run any test. F-065, F-100, F-102, F-121 and F-122 were understated by enough to move up in severity.

If P0 to P3 are read as blocker to low, 19 partials kept that level, 17 fell below it and 9 rose above it.

### Severity miscalibration
| Fable level | Findings | Blocker | High | Medium | Low | Post-MVP | Refuted |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P0 | 9 | 5 | 3 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| P1 | 38 | 9 | 16 | 11 | 0 | 1 | 1 |
| P2 | 53 | 5 | 7 | 14 | 18 | 9 | 0 |
| P3 | 28 | 2 | 2 | 4 | 20 | 0 | 0 |

- **Under-rated.** 7 P2/P3 findings are MVP blockers. They are the Weaviate healthcheck F-052/F-053 (MVP-002), the notification import root F-065 (MVP-006), the EventConsumer offset handling F-087/F-090 (MVP-007), the failing roster lookup F-102 (MVP-011) and the safety LLM stub F-122 (MVP-030). Fable filed F-102 and F-122 as P3 cleanup, yet both break crisis alerting. 5 of these 7 needed an Opus tie-break, so one verifier first agreed with Fable's lower rating. 9 more P2/P3 findings are high, for example F-117 (MVP-052), F-121 (MVP-067), F-048 (MVP-095), F-057 (MVP-083), F-097 (MVP-101) and F-100 (MVP-040).
- **Over-rated.** 4 of 9 P0 findings are not blockers. F-002 audit (MVP-091), F-003 PHI encryption (MVP-055) and F-009 memory writes (MVP-065) are high, and F-008 CI build context (MVP-112) is medium. Of 38 P1 findings, 11 are medium, F-034 is post-MVP because HA is out of scope, and F-044 was refuted. 9 P2 findings are post-MVP because they concern components or features outside pilot scope, such as MFA, data export, ClickHouse, the RBAC engine, the RAG pipeline and single-instance Kafka and WS state.
- **Overall.** If P0 to P3 are read as blocker to low, 55 of the 127 real findings kept their level, 43 moved down and 29 moved up. Some downgrades follow from the pilot scope, and others from code paths that are dead or latent. Most upgrades are on the boot and crisis paths.

### Internal duplication
26 of Fable's 128 findings repeat another Fable finding. Examples: F-001/F-005/F-006 (requirements-runtime.txt, MVP-032), F-019/F-020 (redis_url, MVP-016), F-052/F-053 (Weaviate, MVP-002), F-087/F-090 (EventConsumer, MVP-007), F-028/F-036 (deletion fan-out, MVP-029), F-040/F-015/F-035 (checkpointer, MVP-066), F-008/F-018/F-073 (CI build context, MVP-112), F-062/F-063/F-067 (import roots, MVP-270) and F-093/F-094 (BM25, MVP-278). A list built from Fable's raw findings would count several defects two or three times.

### Blind spots revealed by the new findings
The hunt added 243 upheld entries to Fable's 101. They hold 20 of the 35 blockers and 51 of the 72 highs. By category (Fable vs new):
- **Contract drift: 0 vs 7.** No Fable entry falls in this category. The new entries cover missing credentials (MVP-021), schema and enum drift (MVP-022), user-service INSERTs versus migration 001 (MVP-034), the WS frame format (MVP-072) and the therapy session lifecycle (MVP-078).
- **Correctness: 9 vs 55.** Examples are reducers that replay stale replies (MVP-018), missing chat history (MVP-073), negation-blind emotion scoring (MVP-074), substring topic matching (MVP-075) and diagnosis calibration (MVP-048).
- **Config/env: 3 vs 15.** Examples are MVP-004, MVP-010, MVP-020, MVP-027, MVP-031, MVP-033, MVP-057 and MVP-092.
- **Data integrity: 3 vs 14.** Examples are memory, personality and therapy SQL against the migrated schema (MVP-060, MVP-061, MVP-080, MVP-096) and the non-transactional signup (MVP-106).
- **Security: 9 vs 32. Privacy/PHI: 4 vs 20.** Examples are thread hijack (MVP-070), OAuth pre-account takeover (MVP-102), exposed clinician contacts (MVP-103), the cross-user LLM cache (MVP-056), plaintext PHI in the outbox (MVP-054) and checkpoints with no erase path (MVP-071).
- **Clinical safety: 14 vs 29, with 17 of the new entries blocker or high.** They include detection false negatives (MVP-023, MVP-024, MVP-025, MVP-026, MVP-084), over-escalation (MVP-019, MVP-086) and diagnosis crisis gaps (MVP-046, MVP-047, MVP-049).

By area, Fable has 1 diagnosis entry against 29 new ones, 9 user-service entries against 35, 2 personality entries against 11 and 14 orchestrator entries against 36. Fable spent a larger share on dead code, docs drift and performance: 17 of its 101 entries (17%), against 8 of 243 new entries (3%). 18 of the 20 new blockers would be found by four kinds of check that few Fable entries show. The first is comparing deploy config with what the code reads (MVP-003, MVP-004, MVP-010, MVP-027, MVP-031, MVP-033). The second is tracing a request across a service boundary (MVP-020, MVP-021, MVP-022, MVP-035). The third is reading service SQL against the migrated schema (MVP-009, MVP-034). The fourth is running the graph and the detectors on realistic messages (MVP-018, MVP-019, MVP-023, MVP-024, MVP-025, MVP-026).

## 4. Root-cause clusters

**Images cannot be built or booted from the repository** (12): Dockerfiles, dependency manifests, import roots and compose healthchecks were never exercised by a real build and start. A missing requirements file, unresolvable pins, missing packages, broken relative imports, eager package imports and an inconsistent HEAD stop images from building or starting.

IDs: MVP-002, MVP-005, MVP-006, MVP-008, MVP-012, MVP-013, MVP-032, MVP-199, MVP-248, MVP-269, MVP-270, MVP-310

**Deploy configuration does not match what the code reads** (21): Compose files, .env templates and runbooks set variable names, values, secrets and networks that differ from what each service's settings classes expect. Services fall back to localhost defaults, miss required secrets, or trip their own startup guards under ENVIRONMENT=staging or production.

IDs: MVP-003, MVP-004, MVP-010, MVP-016, MVP-027, MVP-028, MVP-031, MVP-033, MVP-042, MVP-057, MVP-092, MVP-095, MVP-116, MVP-117, MVP-118, MVP-164, MVP-225, MVP-247, MVP-249, MVP-263, MVP-273

**Service SQL and migrations disagree** (11): The Alembic chain cannot be applied and is never run, and the raw SQL in services was written against column names, keys and constraints that the migrations do not create. Reads and writes fail against a migrated database, and startup DDL races or double-encodes data.

IDs: MVP-001, MVP-009, MVP-034, MVP-060, MVP-061, MVP-080, MVP-096, MVP-128, MVP-220, MVP-266, MVP-267

**Event plane drops events without durable retry** (21): The outbox, poller, consumer and DLQ code has gaps at each hop: pollers not started, bridge init errors swallowed, offsets committed on failure, in-memory queues and DLQs, no retry on producer start, unsafe claims and no reaper. Crisis and other domain events can be lost with only a log line.

IDs: MVP-007, MVP-014, MVP-015, MVP-051, MVP-052, MVP-053, MVP-063, MVP-081, MVP-132, MVP-133, MVP-134, MVP-135, MVP-136, MVP-137, MVP-138, MVP-151, MVP-253, MVP-264, MVP-265, MVP-268, MVP-332

**Crisis screening relies on brittle keyword and score logic** (24): Crisis detection at chat ingress, in the safety service and in diagnosis, therapy and memory rests on short keyword lists, regexes without text normalization or sound negation, intent thresholds that skip the LLM, and score fusion that lets fallbacks dilute real signals. It misses common crisis phrasings and over-flags benign text.

IDs: MVP-017, MVP-023, MVP-024, MVP-025, MVP-026, MVP-030, MVP-046, MVP-047, MVP-049, MVP-068, MVP-077, MVP-083, MVP-084, MVP-086, MVP-090, MVP-098, MVP-127, MVP-144, MVP-166, MVP-176, MVP-178, MVP-182, MVP-183, MVP-195

**Detected crises do not reach a human clinician** (25): The path from a detected crisis to a clinician has no working recipient source, fragile delivery fallbacks, inline escalation that cannot authenticate, crisis signals that some services never publish, and no fail-safe when the graph raises. Alerts go to a placeholder, are duplicated, delayed or lost, and nothing records whether a clinician was reached.

IDs: MVP-011, MVP-064, MVP-067, MVP-079, MVP-082, MVP-085, MVP-087, MVP-094, MVP-105, MVP-150, MVP-152, MVP-154, MVP-156, MVP-167, MVP-177, MVP-179, MVP-181, MVP-260, MVP-262, MVP-287, MVP-288, MVP-290, MVP-291, MVP-292, MVP-299

**Inter-service calls use wrong URLs, credentials and schemas** (14): The orchestrator and other callers were written against assumed contracts that the target services do not implement: URL env var names, token types, request and response schemas, enum case, session lifecycles, retries and timeouts. Calls fail and the failure is absorbed.

IDs: MVP-020, MVP-021, MVP-022, MVP-035, MVP-072, MVP-078, MVP-159, MVP-162, MVP-170, MVP-171, MVP-194, MVP-197, MVP-293, MVP-321

**Orchestrator graph state and reply assembly are wrong** (12): LangGraph reducers accumulate state across checkpointed turns, and the chat path omits history, uses substring and negation-blind classifiers, and assembles replies from stale or mismatched data. Replies repeat, misread distress or ignore earlier turns.

IDs: MVP-018, MVP-019, MVP-065, MVP-073, MVP-074, MVP-075, MVP-161, MVP-165, MVP-169, MVP-173, MVP-295, MVP-298

**Failures are swallowed and health stays green** (15): Errors from dependencies, LLM calls, consumers and startup are caught and replaced with templates, empty strings or in-memory fallbacks, and health endpoints do not check dependencies. There are no metrics, alerts, tracebacks or recovery actions, so degradation is invisible to operators.

IDs: MVP-036, MVP-044, MVP-069, MVP-076, MVP-107, MVP-110, MVP-114, MVP-119, MVP-139, MVP-153, MVP-157, MVP-160, MVP-180, MVP-200, MVP-274

**PHI controls exist in code but do not run** (19): Encryption, audit logging, consent and log sanitization are implemented as libraries but are not applied on the write, access and logging paths that carry PHI. PHI is stored, logged and sent to third parties in plaintext, and the regex-only sanitizer that does run also corrupts some IDs or raises.

IDs: MVP-054, MVP-055, MVP-091, MVP-093, MVP-100, MVP-109, MVP-121, MVP-145, MVP-187, MVP-188, MVP-189, MVP-207, MVP-211, MVP-218, MVP-252, MVP-279, MVP-313, MVP-315, MVP-316

**Per-user isolation and authorization checks are missing or wrong** (16): Isolation depends on app-layer checks because services connect as a superuser and the RLS policies are fragile. Those checks are absent, mistyped or caller-scoped in several endpoints, caches and indexes, so users can reach other users' data or are denied their own.

IDs: MVP-041, MVP-056, MVP-058, MVP-059, MVP-070, MVP-103, MVP-120, MVP-141, MVP-143, MVP-193, MVP-234, MVP-280, MVP-285, MVP-297, MVP-309, MVP-314

**Account deletion and data-subject rights do not complete** (12): Deletion in user-service is never propagated, and the per-service erasure code targets missing tables, skips in-memory and derived stores, or cannot be retried. Data-subject rights beyond deletion are absent.

IDs: MVP-029, MVP-062, MVP-071, MVP-089, MVP-097, MVP-172, MVP-198, MVP-208, MVP-216, MVP-229, MVP-281, MVP-343

**Authentication, session and abuse controls are weak** (36): User-service auth flows, token handling and rate limiting have logic gaps: no reset flow, OAuth linking and handoff defects, refresh and lockout bugs, client IP taken from the proxy, blocking crypto on the event loop, and error and docs leakage. Public orchestrator endpoints also lack connection and abuse limits.

IDs: MVP-099, MVP-101, MVP-102, MVP-104, MVP-106, MVP-158, MVP-163, MVP-168, MVP-185, MVP-186, MVP-209, MVP-210, MVP-212, MVP-213, MVP-214, MVP-215, MVP-217, MVP-219, MVP-221, MVP-222, MVP-223, MVP-224, MVP-226, MVP-227, MVP-228, MVP-230, MVP-296, MVP-312, MVP-317, MVP-323, MVP-326, MVP-327, MVP-328, MVP-329, MVP-330, MVP-344

**Process-local state used where durability is needed** (17): Sessions, treatment plans, checkpoints, risk history, caches and counters live in process memory, with silent fallbacks to in-memory repositories and no caps or eviction. State is lost on restart, diverges from the database, and grows without bound.

IDs: MVP-066, MVP-088, MVP-175, MVP-184, MVP-190, MVP-191, MVP-192, MVP-251, MVP-254, MVP-255, MVP-294, MVP-301, MVP-304, MVP-311, MVP-318, MVP-320, MVP-341

**Clinical and memory domain logic is incorrect or unused** (43): Diagnosis, therapy, memory and personality logic contains regex, scoring, phase, retrieval and selection errors, and several computed results are dropped or never called. Outputs are clinically wrong or empty even when the service runs.

IDs: MVP-048, MVP-050, MVP-122, MVP-123, MVP-124, MVP-125, MVP-126, MVP-129, MVP-130, MVP-131, MVP-142, MVP-146, MVP-147, MVP-148, MVP-174, MVP-196, MVP-201, MVP-202, MVP-203, MVP-204, MVP-205, MVP-206, MVP-256, MVP-257, MVP-258, MVP-259, MVP-261, MVP-275, MVP-276, MVP-277, MVP-278, MVP-282, MVP-283, MVP-284, MVP-286, MVP-300, MVP-303, MVP-305, MVP-306, MVP-307, MVP-322, MVP-324, MVP-325

**Tests and CI do not exercise the deployed system** (10): CI has never run a test because a permanent lint failure gates every job, and the suites are in-process and mock-based with gaps in coverage, scanning and test configuration. Defects that need real images or infrastructure are never caught.

IDs: MVP-038, MVP-039, MVP-112, MVP-113, MVP-149, MVP-155, MVP-243, MVP-244, MVP-245, MVP-246

**Single-host infrastructure exposure, capacity and backup gaps** (10): The single compose host and its AWS template publish internal ports, admit no inbound traffic, cap memory below process needs, open enough DB pools to nearly exhaust Postgres connections and keep all data on one deletable volume with no backup. The edge does no rate limiting and DB TLS does not verify certificates.

IDs: MVP-037, MVP-040, MVP-043, MVP-045, MVP-115, MVP-140, MVP-250, MVP-271, MVP-335, MVP-336

**Other: analytics, dead code, docs drift and out-of-scope components** (26): Analytics ingests nothing and computes wrong or empty metrics, several modules are dead or unreachable, docs and TODOs are stale, and some findings concern components outside MVP scope such as config-service, ClickHouse, the RBAC engine, the RAG pipeline and push.

IDs: MVP-108, MVP-111, MVP-231, MVP-232, MVP-233, MVP-235, MVP-236, MVP-237, MVP-238, MVP-239, MVP-240, MVP-241, MVP-242, MVP-272, MVP-289, MVP-302, MVP-308, MVP-319, MVP-331, MVP-333, MVP-334, MVP-337, MVP-338, MVP-339, MVP-340, MVP-342

**Possible remaining overlaps.** Deduplication ran across 438 items. These register entries may describe the same defect from different angles. They are kept separate here; confirm them during planning.

- MVP-005, MVP-012: Both report that the notification image lacks email-validator, so pydantic EmailStr fails at import.
- MVP-005, MVP-032: Both stem from requirements-runtime.txt; MVP-005 covers the pin conflicts that remain once the missing file is recreated.
- MVP-001, MVP-009: Both cite migration 007's unguarded CREATE INDEX on event_outbox rolling back the single upgrade transaction.
- MVP-003, MVP-014, MVP-028: All three explain why the orchestrator's crisis event never reaches Kafka: wrong network, no poller, event plane disabled.
- MVP-015, MVP-053: Both describe a Kafka producer start failure at boot leaving the bridge absent for the process lifetime with no retry.
- MVP-015, MVP-069: Both describe the orchestrator losing crisis events silently after bridge init fails, while logs and /health look normal.
- MVP-013, MVP-016: Both make the orchestrator crash at import in production through the rate limiter; MVP-016 notes that the missing infrastructure/ copy fails first.
- MVP-028, MVP-052: Both rest on fail-loud guards that fire only for ENVIRONMENT=production while the documented deploy runs staging.
- MVP-033, MVP-052, MVP-088: All three trace to the default Postgres pool resolving to localhost because POSTGRES_HOST is unset in the containers.
- MVP-007, MVP-138: Both describe EventConsumer committing the offset after a failure in the handler path, so a crisis event is never redelivered.
- MVP-051, MVP-063: Both report dead-lettered crisis alerts sitting in a non-durable DLQ that nothing retries.
- MVP-081, MVP-132: Both describe the in-process asyncio.Queue between the safety domain write and the outbox, with swallowed save errors.
- MVP-136, MVP-332: Both leave claimed outbox rows stuck in PUBLISHING for the 300 s stale window after a cancelled flush.
- MVP-021, MVP-065: The memory read-path 401 in MVP-065 is the missing Authorization header described in MVP-021.
- MVP-020, MVP-021, MVP-076: MVP-076's scenario is the localhost URL and missing-credential failures of MVP-020 and MVP-021, seen from the fallback side.
- MVP-023, MVP-166: Both describe the safety agent's weak substring fallback list used when the safety service is unreachable.
- MVP-024, MVP-077: Both describe keyword intent confidence at or above 0.6 skipping the LLM classifier, so crisis messages route to chat.
- MVP-046, MVP-127: Both report the diagnosis risk regex failing to match 'suicidal'/'suicide' and raising false positives on bare keywords.
- MVP-086, MVP-182: Both report benign 'cutting' text scoring HIGH in the safety service and auto-escalating.
- MVP-030, MVP-057: Both report that the deployed LLM provider key is never read, so LLM calls fail and fall back silently (different clients).
- MVP-055, MVP-218: Both report PHI stored in plaintext because the encryption code is never applied to raw SQL writes.
- MVP-093, MVP-108, MVP-109: All three describe analytics failing on every Kafka event and logging each raw event, PHI included, past the regex sanitizer.
- MVP-029, MVP-071: Both rest on user.deleted never being published or consumed; MVP-071 adds that orchestrator checkpoints have no erase path.
- MVP-029, MVP-089: Both cover the safety erasure module, which nothing calls and which targets tables no migration creates.
- MVP-060, MVP-062: Both hit the missing user_facts table in memory-service Postgres.
- MVP-066, MVP-341: Both describe orchestrator checkpoints held only in process memory through InMemorySaver.
- MVP-018, MVP-019: Same mechanism: accumulating reducers over checkpointed state, applied to agent_results and to safety_flags.
- MVP-078, MVP-197: Both report therapy returning HTTP 200 with an empty response_text on SESSION_NOT_FOUND.
- MVP-087, MVP-159: Both describe orchestrator retries of non-idempotent safety /check calls that produce duplicate crisis events.
- MVP-181, MVP-299: Both describe one HIGH/CRITICAL message producing up to three uncorrelated clinician alerts.
- MVP-011, MVP-105: Both leave the on-call recipient list empty, so every crisis alert goes to the placeholder fallback address.
- MVP-101, MVP-326: Both stem from user-service taking Caddy's container IP as the client IP.
- MVP-107, MVP-118: Both describe REDIS_PASSWORD enabling requirepass while user-service's client lacks it and silently drops to in-memory state.
- MVP-036, MVP-044: Both report that no alerting exists: no Prometheus rules, Alertmanager or host alarms.
- MVP-037, MVP-043, MVP-335: All three report PHI volumes on a single root EBS volume with DeleteOnTermination and no backup.
- MVP-038, MVP-112: Both rest on the always-red lint gate that skips every downstream CI job, including the image build.
- MVP-215, MVP-227: Both report login_attempts never being reset after a lockout, which keeps accounts locked.
- MVP-006, MVP-270: Both concern the REV-31 dual import roots; MVP-006 is the case that crashes notification-service.
- MVP-189, MVP-313: Both report the case-insensitive licence regex masking a segment of about 1.6-1.7% of UUIDs.
- MVP-168, MVP-296: Both report /batch returning raw str(exception) text to the caller.

Ordering: severity, then area.

- **F-xxx:** a Fable finding. Any internal Fable duplicates are listed after it.
- **N-xxx:** a new finding.
- **+k:** the number of hunter reports merged in.

| N-147 | GET /sessions/{id}/history reads the checkpointer with a synchronous .get in a thread, so switching to the AsyncPostgresSaver turns every history call into a silent empty 200 | C: The headline mechanism is backwards.
